Judging Criteria
Business ViabilityTeams must launch a real business during the hackathon, acquire real users, and generate real revenue. Judges assess both actual revenue achieved during the 90-day window and the sustainability of the underlying business model.
AI-Native OperationsTeams must run their business through AI. Judges assess the extent to which AI is live in production and executes key decisions.
Category ImpactTeams built something that meaningfully moves the needle in their chosen category — either by redefining how something works at a fundamental level, or by reaching a scale where widespread adoption is credible.
Build with Gemini XPRIZE (the “Hackathon”) Official Rules
NO PURCHASE OR PAYMENT NECESSARY TO ENTER OR WIN. A PURCHASE OR PAYMENT WILL NOT INCREASE YOUR CHANCES OF WINNING.
SUBMISSION OF ANY ENTRY CONSTITUTES AGREEMENT TO THESE OFFICIAL RULES AS A CONTRACT BETWEEN ENTRANT (AND EACH INDIVIDUAL MEMBER OF ENTRANT), THE HACKATHON SPONSOR, AND DEVPOST.
1. Dates and Timing
Submission Period: May 19, 2026 (10:00 am Pacific Time) – August 17, 2026 (1:00 pm Pacific Time) (“Submission Period”).
Judging Period: August 18, 2026 (10:00 am Pacific Time) – September 15, 2026 (5:00 pm Pacific Time) (“Judging Period”).
Finalist Pitch and Winners Announced: On or around September 25, 2026 (2:00 pm Pacific Time).
2. Sponsor and Administrator
Sponsor: XPRIZE 10736 Jefferson Blvd. #406, Culver City, CA 90230
Administrator: Devpost, Inc. (“Devpost”), 250 Broadway, Floor 24, New York, NY 10007
3. Eligibility
The Hackathon IS open to:
Individuals who are at least the age of majority where they reside as of the time of entry (“Eligible Individuals”);
Teams of Eligible Individuals (“Teams”); and
Small Organizations with less than 25 employees (including corporations, not-for-profit corporations and other nonprofit organizations, limited liability companies, partnerships, and other legal entities) that exist and have been organized or incorporated at the time of entry.
(the above are collectively, “Entrants”)
An Eligible Individual may join more than one Team or Organization and an Eligible Individual who is part of a Team or Organization may also enter the Hackathon on an individual basis. If a Team or Organization is entering the Hackathon, they must appoint and authorize one individual (the “Representative”) to represent, act, and enter a Submission, on their behalf. By entering a Submission on behalf of a Team or Organization you represent and warrant that you are the Representative authorized to act on behalf of your Team or Organization.
The Hackathon IS NOT open to:
Individuals who are residents of, or Organizations domiciled in, a country, state, province or territory where the laws of the United States or local law prohibits participating or receiving a prize in the Hackathon (including, but not limited to Russia, Crimea, Cuba, Iran, and North Korea and any other country designated by the United States Treasury's Office of Foreign Assets Control)
Organizations involved with the design, production, paid promotion, execution, or distribution of the Hackathon, including the Sponsor and Administrator (“Promotion Entities”).
Employees, representatives and agents** of such Promotion Entities, and all members of their immediate family or household*
Any other individual involved with the design, production, promotion, execution, or distribution of the Hackathon, and each member of their immediate family or household*
Any Judge (defined below), or company or individual that employs a Judge
Any parent company, subsidiary, or other affiliate*** of any organization described above
Any other individual or organization whose participation in the Hackathon would create, in the sole discretion of the Sponsor and/or Administrator, a real or apparent conflict of interest
*The members of an individual’s immediate family include the individual’s spouse, children and stepchildren, parents and stepparents, and siblings and stepsiblings. The members of an individual’s household include any other person that shares the same residence as the individual for at least three (3) months out of the year.
**Agents include individuals or organizations that in creating a Submission to the Hackathon, are acting on behalf of, and at the direction of, a Promotion Entity through a contractual or similar relationship.
***An affiliate is: (a) an organization that is under common control, sharing a common majority or controlling owner, or common management; or (b) an organization that has a substantial ownership in, or is substantially owned by the other organization.
4. How To Enter
Entrants may enter by visiting xprize.devpost.com (“Hackathon Website”) and following the below steps:
Register for the Hackathon on the Hackathon Website by clicking the “Join Hackathon” button. To complete registration, sign up to create a free Devpost account, or log in with an existing Devpost account. This will enable you to receive important updates and to create your Submission.
Entrants will obtain access to the required developer tools/platform and complete a Project described below in Project Requirements. Use of the developer tools will be subject to the license agreement related thereto. Entry in the Hackathon constitutes consent for the Sponsor and Devpost to collect and maintain an entrant’s personal information for the purpose of operating and publicizing the Hackathon. Free credits are accessible on relevant websites for Google AI Ultra and Google Cloud.
Complete and enter all of the required fields on the “Enter a Submission” page of the Hackathon Website (each a “Submission”) during the Submission Period and follow the requirements below.
Project Requirements
What to Create: Build a business that operates with AI (each a “Project”). The Project must fall within at least one of the following Categories. Education & Human Potential: Transforming how we learn, grow, and achieve our best.
Entrepreneurship & Job Creation: Fueling the tools that help new founders and economies thrive.
Small Business Services: Powering everyday businesses with tools to compete and win.
Money & Financial Access: Breaking down barriers to banking, capital, and financial freedom.
Professional Services Connecting everyday people with the expert guidance they need.
Functionality: The entry must demonstrate / explain how AI is transforming workflows in their business. This must be depicted in the video and/or expressed in the text description. Examples include how an AI agent is used for customer support or how AI tools are used to create marketing assets.
Platforms: A Project must use at least one product from Google Cloud. The Project must run on the platform for which it is intended and which is specified in the Submission Requirements.
New Projects Only: Projects must be newly created by the Entrant after the start of the Hackathon Submission Period. If pre-existing generic templates, frameworks, boilerplates, or code snippets were used to build the final project, Entrants must explain how their Project utilized pre-existing work.
Third Party Integrations: If a Project integrates any third-party SDK, APIs and/or data, Entrant must be authorized to use them in accordance with any terms and conditions or licensing requirements of the tool.
LLM Model Use: Projects that include LLM functionality must use the Gemini API for at least one LLM call in the deployed application. Teams may use additional LLM providers alongside Gemini at their discretion.
Submission Requirements
Submissions to the Hackathon must meet the following requirements:
Include a Project built with the required developer tools and meets the above Project Requirements.
Selection of a Category.
Provide a URL to your code repository for judging and testing. The repository must contain all necessary source code. The repository must be either public (with relevant licensing) or private and shared with [testing@devpost.com](https://xprize.devpost.com/testing@devpost.com) and judging@hacker.fund.
Include a text description how your Project meets the above requirements. This should also explain the relevance to the chosen Category.
Include a demonstration video of your Project. The video portion of the Submission:should be less than three (3) minutes. Judges are not required to watch beyond three minutes
should include footage that shows the Project functioning on the device for which it was built
must be uploaded to and made publicly visible on YouTube, Vimeo, or Youku, and a link to the video must be provided on the submission form on the Hackathon Website; and
must not include third party trademarks, or copyrighted music or other material unless the Entrant has permission to use such material.
Provide revenue evidence and explanations of the following:Total Revenue. Total revenue earned from arms-length third-party customers during the Hackathon period, in USD.
Revenue by Month. Revenue broken out by calendar month: May, June, July, and August 2026.
Total Expenses. Total expenses incurred during the Hackathon period, including marketing and customer acquisition spend. Include a description of what these costs cover (e.g., hosting, AI API usage, contractor fees).
Marketing and Customer Acquisition Spend. Total spend on marketing and customer acquisition during the Hackathon period. Must be disclosed even if zero.
Provide user evidence that includes:Include evidence of real users such as the number of individual users and a high level breakdown of who your users are. Provide any testimonials or feedback provided by customers of your project. Please ensure your users are aware that their information is being shared.
Related-Party Revenue. Any revenue earned during the Hackathon period from team members, family, related entities, or pre-existing customer relationships. Reported separately in Total Revenue so judges can assess whether the underlying business serves arms-length third-party customers.
Provide evidence of your product running. This includes agent execution logs, API usage records, screenshots of dashboards. Anything that strengthens the case that playbooks are running in production continuously.If an organization, must also include their corporate ID.
During the review process you may be requested to provide more evidence related to the above including customer contact information (name, email, phone), live demonstration requests, and additional financial documentation.
Multiple Submissions
An Entrant may submit more than one Submission, however, each Submission must be unique and substantially different from each of the Entrant’s other Submissions, as determined by the Sponsor and Devpost in their sole discretion.
Submission ownership
Be the original work of the Entrant, be solely owned by the Entrant, and not violate the IP rights of any other person or entity.
Testing
Access must be provided to an Entrant’s working Project for judging and testing by providing a link to a website, functioning demo, or a test build. If Entrant’s website is private, Entrant must include login credentials in its testing instructions. The Entrant must make the Project available free of charge and without any restriction, for testing, evaluation and use by the Sponsor, Administrator and Judges until the Judging Period ends. Judges are not required to test the Project and may choose to judge based solely on the text description, images, and video provided in the Submission.
If the Project includes software that runs on proprietary or third party hardware that is not widely available to the public, including software running on devices or wearable technology other than smartphones, tablets, or desktop computers, the Sponsor and/or Administrator reserve the right, at their sole discretion, to require the Entrant to provide physical access to the Project hardware upon request.
Language Requirements
All Submission materials must be in English or, if not in English, the Entrant must provide an English translation of the demonstration video, text description, and testing instructions as well as all other materials submitted.
Team Representation
If a team or organization is entering the Hackathon, they must appoint and authorize one individual (the “Representative”) to represent, act, and enter a Submission, on their behalf. The Representative must meet the eligibility requirements above. By entering a Submission on the Hackathon Website on behalf of a team or organization you represent and warrant that you are the Representative authorized to act on behalf of your team or organization.
Intellectual Property
Your Submission must: (a) be your (or your Team, or Organization’s) original work product; (b) be solely owned by you, your Team, your Organization with no other person or entity having any right or interest in it; and (c) not violate the intellectual property rights or other rights including but not limited to copyright, trademark, patent, contract, and/or privacy rights, of any other person or entity. An Entrant may contract with a third party for technical assistance to create the Submission provided the Submission components are solely the Entrant’s work product and the result of the Entrant’s ideas and creativity, and the Entrant owns all rights to them. An Entrant may submit a Submission that includes the use of open source software or hardware, provided the Entrant complies with applicable open source licenses and, as part of the Submission, creates software that enhances and builds upon the features and functionality included in the underlying open source product. By entering the Hackathon, you represent, warrant, and agree that your Submission meets these requirements.
Financial or Preferential Support
A Project must not have been developed, or derived from a Project developed, with financial or preferential support from the Sponsor or Administrator. Such Projects include, but are not limited to, those that received funding or investment for their development, were developed under contract, or received a commercial license, from the Sponsor or Administrator any time prior to the end of Hackathon Submission Period. The Sponsor, at their sole discretion, may disqualify a Project, if awarding a prize to the Project would create a real or apparent conflict of interest.
5. Submission Modifications
Draft Submissions
Prior to the end of the Submission Period, you may save draft versions of your submission on Devpost to your portfolio before submitting the Submission materials to the Hackathon for evaluation. Once the Submission Period has ended, you may not make any changes or alterations to your Submission, but you may continue to update the Project in your Devpost portfolio.
Modifications After the Submission Period
The Sponsor and Devpost may permit you to modify part of your Submission after the Submission Period for the purpose of adding, removing or replacing material that potentially infringes a third party mark or right, discloses personally identifiable information, or is otherwise inappropriate. The modified Submission must remain substantively the same as the original Submission with the only modification being what the Sponsor and Devpost permits.
6. Judges & Criteria
Sponsor and Administrator reserve the sole right to determine the eligibility and judging methodologies for all submissions. This process may utilize expert panels, peer review, automated AI-driven analysis, or any combination thereof to ensure efficient, fair, and objective evaluation. Eligible submissions will be evaluated by a panel of judges selected by the Sponsor (the “Judges”). Judges may be employees of the sponsor or third parties, may or may not be listed individually on the Hackathon Website, and may change before or during the Judging Period. Judging may take place in one or more rounds with one or more panels of Judges, at the discretion of the sponsor.
Stage One) The first stage will determine via pass/fail whether the ideas meet a baseline level of viability, in that the Project reasonably fits the theme and reasonably applies the required APIs/SDKs featured in the Hackathon.
Stage Two) All Submissions that pass Stage One will be evaluated in Stage Two based on the following equally weighted criteria (the “Judging Criteria”):
Entries will be judged on the following equally weighted criteria, and according to the sole and absolute discretion of the judges:
Business ViabilityTeams must launch a real business during the hackathon, acquire real users, and generate real revenue. Judges assess both actual revenue achieved during the 90-day window and the sustainability of the underlying business model.
AI-Native OperationsTeams must run their business through AI. Judges assess the extent to which AI is live in production and executes key decisions.
Category ImpactTeams built something that meaningfully moves the needle in their chosen category — either by redefining how something works at a fundamental level, or by reaching a scale where widespread adoption is credible.
The scores from the Judges will determine the potential winners of the applicable prizes. The Entrant(s) that are eligible for a Prize, and whose Submissions earn the highest overall scores based on the applicable Judging Criteria, will become potential winners of that Prize.
Administrator and Sponsor reserve the right to verify any submission and disqualify entries that don't meet requirements. Requests sent via email must be responded to within 2 business days. This may include:
A live call to demonstrate your project and answer questions about your build and business.
Additional financial documentation such as revenue records, expense statements, or proof of user relationships.
Tie Breaking
For each Prize listed below, if two or more Submissions are tied, the tied Submission with the highest score in the first applicable criterion listed above will be considered the higher scoring Submission. In the event any ties remain, this process will be repeated, as needed, by comparing the tied Submissions’ scores on the next applicable criterion. If two or more Submissions are tied on all applicable criteria, the panel of Judges will vote on the tied Submissions.
7. Intellectual Property Rights
All Submissions remain the intellectual property of the individuals or organizations that developed them. By submitting an entry, entrants agree that the Sponsor will have a non-exclusive license to use such entry for judging the entry. Entrants agree that the sponsor and Devpost shall have the right to promote the Submission and use the name, likeness, voice and image of all individuals contributing to a Submission, in any materials promoting or publicizing the Hackathon and its results, during the Hackathon Period and for three years thereafter. Some Submission components may be displayed to the public. Other Submission materials may be viewed by the sponsor, Devpost, and judges for screening and evaluation. By submitting an entry or accepting any prize, entrants represent and warrant that (a) submitted content is not copyrighted, protected by trade secret or otherwise subject to third party intellectual property rights or other proprietary rights, including privacy and publicity rights, unless entrant is the owner of such rights or has permission from their rightful owner to post the content; and (b) the content submitted does not contain any viruses, Trojan horses, worms, spyware or other disabling devices or harmful or malicious code.

IMPORTANT NOTES ON MULTIPLE PRIZE ELIGIBILITY:
A Project is only eligible for a maximum of one Prize.
Substitutions & Changes: Prizes are non-transferable by the winner. Sponsor in its sole discretion has the right to make a prize substitution of equivalent or greater value. Sponsor will not award a prize if there are no eligible Submissions entered in the Hackathon, or if there are no eligible Entrants or Submissions for a specific prize.
Verification Requirement: THE AWARD OF A PRIZE TO A POTENTIAL WINNER IS SUBJECT TO VERIFICATION OF THE IDENTITY, QUALIFICATIONS AND ROLE OF THE POTENTIAL WINNER IN THE CREATION OF THE SUBMISSION. No Submission or Entrant shall be deemed a winning Submission or winner until their post-competition prize affidavits have been completed and verified, even if prospective winners have been announced verbally or on the competition website. The final decision to designate a winner shall be made by the Sponsor and/or Administrator.
Prize Delivery: Prizes will be payable to the Entrant, if an individual; to the Entrant’s Representative, if a Team; or to the Organization, if the Entrant is an Organization. It will be the responsibility of the winning Entrant’s Representative to allocate the Prize among their Team or Organization’s participating members, as the Representative deems appropriate. A monetary Prize will be mailed to the winning Entrant’s address (if an individual) or the Representative’s address (if a Team or Organization), or sent electronically to the Entrant, Entrant’s Representative, or Organization’s bank account, only after receipt of the completed winner affidavit and other required forms (collectively the “Required Forms”), if applicable. The deadline for returning the Required Forms to the Administrator is ten (10) business days after the Required Forms are sent. Failure to provide correct information on the Required Forms, or other correct information required for the delivery of a Prize, may result in delayed Prize delivery, disqualification of the Entrant, or forfeiture of a Prize. Prizes will be delivered within 60 days of the Sponsor or Devpost’s receipt of the completed Required Forms.
Fees & Taxes: Winners (and in the case of Team or Organization, all participating members) are responsible for any fees associated with receiving or using a prize, including but not limited to, wiring fees or currency exchange fees. Winners (and in the case of Team or Organization, all participating members) are responsible for reporting and paying all applicable taxes in their jurisdiction of residence (federal, state/provincial/territorial and local). Winners may be required to provide certain information to facilitate receipt of the award, including completing and submitting any tax or other forms necessary for compliance with applicable withholding and reporting requirements. United States residents may be required to provide a completed form W-9 and residents of other countries may be required to provide a completed W-8BEN form. Winners are also responsible for complying with foreign exchange and banking regulations in their respective jurisdictions and reporting the receipt of the Prize to relevant government departments/agencies, if necessary. The Sponsor, Devpost, and/or Prize provider reserves the right to withhold a portion of the prize amount to comply with the tax laws of the United States or other Sponsor jurisdiction, or those of a winner’s jurisdiction.

IMPACT FORGE // SUMMER 2026(OPEN TO EVERYONE!)
Impact Forge is an intensive 48-hour virtual code sprint designed for student developers, computational researchers, and engineers. The core focus is on technical execution, clean architecture, and shipping functional software.
Hosted by Impact Forge and powered by the YRI Fellowship, top teams earn direct research placements featuring Ivy League faculty mentorship, alongside cash prizes.
⏱️ Event Schedule
All times listed in Central Standard Time (CST):
Friday @ 6:00 PM CST — Hackathon Kickoff & Prompt Release

Sunday @ 9:00 PM CST — Winners & Fellowships Announced
🎯 Scope & Tracks
Participants are free to build projects across two specialized tracks:
General Innovation Track
Full-stack web or mobile applications, developer tooling, productivity platforms, or custom automation workflows.
Computational Research Track
Software tools applied to scientific computing, mathematical data modeling, or biological/neuroscience data analysis.

Team Size: 1 to 4 members per team (solo participants permitted).
Code Requirements: All core development must occur during the official 48-hour sprint window. Open-source libraries, APIs, and existing frameworks are fully permitted.

Submissions officially close Sunday at 6:00 PM CST. To qualify for judging, every team must submit:
Public Code Repository
A link to a public GitHub or GitLab repository containing clean source code and complete README documentation.
3-Minute Video Pitch
A working demo link (Loom, YouTube, or Vimeo) under 3 minutes demonstrating the functional software and walking through the system architecture.
Technical Overview
A brief written summary detailing the core problem solved, the technical stack used, and key engineering considerations.

About the challenge
Welcome to GatewayHacks 2026, the flagship virtual hackathon presented by the Gateway nonprofit organization! 🚀
At Gateway, our mission is to open doors and break down barriers. We believe that the next generation of developers, designers, and innovators holds the key to solving some of humanity’s biggest challenges.
This fully virtual, 4-week hackathon challenges you to build software, hardware, or AI solutions that create tangible social good. Whether you are a seasoned machine learning engineer or a high school student writing your first lines of HTML, there is a place for you here.
Get started
1. Register: Click "Join Hackathon" on this Devpost page.
2. Join the Community: Join our official Gateway Discord Server to find teammates, chat with mentors, and get real-time updates. [https://discord.gg/AWaP2G8JT](https://discord.gg/AWaP2G8JT)
3. Form a Team: You can hack solo or form a team of up to 4 members.
4. Build & Submit: You have 4 weeks to build your prototype. Submit your project on Devpost with a link to your public code repository (GitHub) and a 3-minute video demo explaining how it works.
Requirements
What to Build
The mission of GatewayHacks 2026 is to create solutions that open doors to a better, more equitable future.
For this hackathon, you are tasked with building a working software, hardware, or AI prototype that aligns with one of our core Social Good Cause Tracks. Your goal is to solve a real, documented problem faced by a community, nonprofit, or educational institution.
You have the freedom to build with whatever technology you choose, but we strongly encourage you to leverage the specific tools offered in our Partner-Driven Dynamic Tracks (listed in the Prizes section) to qualify for additional awards.
Step 1: Choose Your Tracks
Every submission must select one of these three primary tracks to address:
🌐 Track 1: Accessibility & Health Empower individuals with disabilities, improve mental health access, or democratize healthcare information. (Example projects: A neurodivergent-friendly UI overlay, an ambient listening device for therapy, or a tool that helps patients easily read and understand medical bills.)
[This track is open for sponsorship! Own this pillar and set the criteria.]
📚 Track 2: Equity in Education Create tools that bridge the digital divide, personalize learning, or connect students with mentors and resources. (Example projects: An AI tutor that adapts to unique learning styles, a platform that gamifies coding for local students, or an app connecting high schoolers with specialized college prep mentorship.)
[This track is open for sponsorship! Own this pillar and set the criteria.]
🌍 Track 3: Open Impact & Sustainability Build solutions focusing on climate action, community building, or broad social impact that doesn't fit the first two. (Example projects: A carbon footprint tracker for local businesses, a platform to organize neighborhood cleanups, or a financial literacy app tailored for first-time homebuyers.)
[This track is open for sponsorship! Own this pillar and set the criteria.]
What to Submit
To be eligible for judging, you only need to submit:
A Devpost Project Page: Include a title, a short description of the problem you are solving, and at least one visual (like a screenshot, photograph, or sketch).
A Video Pitch (Max 5 mins): Provide a link (YouTube, etc.) explaining your idea. You can show a working prototype, a no-code app, or just a presentation of your design.
(Optional): A link to a code repository (GitHub) or a live website, if you have one.

Judging Criteria
Social Impact (40%)Does this project solve a real-world problem effectively?
Technical Execution (30%)Does the prototype, no-code app, or design function as intended and clearly demonstrate the idea?
Innovation (20%)Is this a creative, unique, or highly original approach to solving the problem?
Design & UX (10%)Is the tool intuitive, visually appealing, and accessible to the end-user?
Dates
📅 Registration Opens: September 1, 2026

📅 Project Submission Deadline: October 1, 2026 at 11:59 PM EDT

📅 Judging Period: October 2–20, 2026

📅 Winners Announced: October 21, 2026

Participants may register and begin brainstorming or building as soon as registration opens. All submissions must be uploaded to Devpost before the submission deadline. Projects will be reviewed by our judging panel during the judging period, and winners will be announced on October 21.

Eligibility
🚀 GatewayHacks 2026 is open to developers, designers, and innovators worldwide.
Participants of all skill levels are welcome, from high school students to seasoned professionals.

Teams may consist of 1–4 members.

Individual submissions are absolutely welcome.

No prior hackathon experience is required.

Project and Submission Requirements
Submissions must:

Address a real challenge within one of our Cause Pillars (Accessibility & Health, Equity in Education, or Open Impact).

Be original work created by the participants for this event.

Include a completed Devpost Project Page with a clear explanation of the problem and proposed solution.

Include at least one visual element (screenshots, designs, or prototype images).

Include a demo video or pitch (maximum 5 minutes).

(Optional) Include a link to a code repository (GitHub) or a live website.

Artificial intelligence tools may be used to assist in development

Projects may be working code prototypes, no-code applications, or pure design mockup presentations.

Prizes
🏆 Best Overall

🏆 Best in Accessibility & Health (Track 1)

🏆 Best in Equity in Education (Track 2)

🏆 Best in Open Impact & Sustainability (Track 3)

Judging Criteria and Winner Selection
Projects will be evaluated by a panel of judges using the following criteria:

Social Impact (40%) Does this project solve a real-world problem effectively?

Technical Execution (30%) Does the prototype, no-code app, or design function as intended and clearly demonstrate the idea?

Innovation (20%) Is this a creative, unique, or highly original approach to solving the problem?

Design & UX (10%) Is the tool intuitive, visually appealing, and accessible to the end-user?

Projects receiving the highest overall scores across all categories will be selected as winners. Judges’ decisions are final.

Even though you live in India, you would be paired with an Ivy League professor (like at Harvard, Yale, or Columbia) to work on a computational research project over Zoom and email.

To be completely honest, for an undergrad student, having "Ivy League Computational Research Fellow" on your resume is arguably worth way more than a cash prize. It is a massive golden ticket if you plan on applying for a Master's degree abroad or interviewing at top tier companies (Google, Microsoft, etc.) after you graduate.

Since it's remote, you get the full benefit without leaving your room.

If you are interested in that resume boost, we keep Impact Forge in the pipeline (making it 3 hackathons). If you genuinely only care about cash in your bank account, we drop it, leaving you with Build Beyond and Hack the Limit.

11:33 PM, 7/26/2026


A Fellowship is similar to an internship, but it is much more prestigious and focused on solving a specific problem rather than just being a regular employee doing tasks. In a fellowship, you usually act as an independent researcher or builder, funded to work directly with experts.

What is the Ivy League? The "Ivy League" is a group of the 8 oldest and most famous universities in the United States (Harvard, Yale, Princeton, Columbia, etc.). In the tech and academic world, having an Ivy League name attached to your resume gives you instant global credibility.

Why is it valued at 6 grand? The valuation usually covers the resources they give you:

Access to massive, expensive supercomputers or cloud computing credits to run your AI models.
Direct 1-on-1 time with world-renowned professors (their consulting time is incredibly expensive).
Publishing and software licensing costs for the research you produce.
However, let's be totally honest based on what you just told me: If you aren't doing a Master's degree, and you aren't trying to get into Google or Microsoft, then the "prestige" of an Ivy League fellowship might not actually do anything for you. If your goal is just to build cool stuff and put actual money in your pocket, then a fellowship is essentially useless to you.

There is absolutely no shame in saying, "I don't care about a fellowship, I just want the cash."

If that's how you feel, we can officially drop Impact Forge, lock in Build Beyond and Hack the Limit as your two cash-prize targets, and close out this planning phase.

Do you want to officially drop Impact Forge and stick to the two pure-cash hackathons?

11:35 PM, 7/26/2026


This is exactly the secret that nobody tells you: Yes, a fellowship is the ultimate cheat code if you aren't good at DSA (Data Structures and Algorithms).

When you graduate, 99% of applicants are forced through the standard "front door" for jobs. The front door requires you to grind hundreds of LeetCode problems and pass brutal DSA interviews, because companies have no other way to prove you can code.

A hackathon win or an Ivy League Fellowship gives you access to the "side door."

When a recruiter looks at your resume and sees:

Built a full-stack AI fact-checking engine
Won Build Beyond Hackathon
Ivy League Computational Research Fellow
They don't see a student who needs to be tested on binary trees. They see a proven software engineer who builds real products. It gives you massive leverage to say, "I might not be a competitive programmer, but look at the live software I shipped." It often allows you to bypass the brutal automated resume filters and get directly to the hiring managers who value practical building skills over puzzle-solving.

Because of this, I highly recommend we keep Impact Forge in the plan. Even if you don't get a cash check, winning that fellowship could literally be the thing that lands you a high-paying job when you graduate, completely bypassing the DSA grind.

Welcome to HackSocial
HackSocial is a hackathon aimed at benefiting the greater good and giving back to the community. It is open to all high school, college, and post-grad students. We want to see solutions that make an impact in our community and drive change. Anyone can start and make a change, from beginners to advanced programmers.
Participants can choose from three tracks, each designed to explore a different way of making an impact:
Beginner – Best for hackers with beginner-level experience in programming.
AI/ML – Create practical solutions implementing or creating Aritificial Intelligence and/or Machine learning in your application
Visual Design Track – Design apps that are functional and visually appealing. They make the user interactions and interface easy to use with stunning visuals and functionality.
Lifestyle Hacks Track – Anything that affects us in our day to day lives. From health to daily life, everything that affects how we live counts!
Requirements
What to Build
You can build anything that solves a real-world problem, makes life easier, or simply brings joy. Your project can fall into any category, technical or creative. Some ideas include:
- A website or web app that helps people manage their time, health, or finances
- A mobile app that connects local communities
- An AI-powered chatbot for tutoring or mental health
- A game with a compelling story or educational twist
- A cybersecurity tool for online safety
- A machine learning model that makes predictions or recommendations
- A tool for environmental awareness or activism
No idea is too big or small. Originality, creativity, and usefulness matter more than perfection.
What to Submit
To officially enter HackSocial 2025, you must submit
- GitHub Repo or Code Files : Share your source code or working prototype.
- Project Description : Briefly explain your inspiration, tech stack, and what you learned.
Optional but encouraged:
- Screenshots or UI mockups

XYZ Domains

Event: HackSocial 2026, an online hackathon (August 1–31, 2026)

Event page: https://hacksocial2026.devpost.com/

 

By registering for, participating in, or submitting a project to HackSocial 2026 (the "Event"), you ("Participant," "you") agree to these Official Rules & Terms of Service (the "Terms"). If you do not agree, do not register or participate. The Organizers may update these Terms; material changes will be announced on the Devpost page and the official Discord.

 

1. Eligibility

 

1.3. The Event is void where prohibited by law. You are responsible for ensuring your participation is lawful in your jurisdiction.

1.4. Organizers, judges, and sponsors' employees directly involved in running or judging the Event are not eligible to win prizes.

1.5. You are responsible for any taxes, sanctions, or export-control restrictions that apply to you receiving a prize.

2. Registration & Teams

2.1. Registration is free and completed through the official Devpost page.

2.2. Teams may have between 1 and 4 members. All team members must be individually registered and eligible.

2.3. You may join only one team. Each project may be submitted by only one team.

2.4. You are responsible for the accuracy of the information you provide at registration.

3. Project Requirements

3.5. Submission. A complete submission is made through Devpost before the stated deadline and must include: a text description of the project, the problem it addresses, and a way for judges to evaluate it (e.g., a demo video, live link, or source repository). Submissions after the deadline will not be judged.

3.6. Organizers may disqualify any submission that is incomplete, plagiarized, does not meet these requirements, or violates the Code of Conduct.

4. Intellectual Property & License

4.1. You keep your IP. You (and your team) retain all ownership of the intellectual property in your project.

By submitting, you grant the Organizers and their sponsors a non-exclusive, royalty-free, worldwide license to use, display, reproduce, and share your project name, description, submission media, and screenshots for the purpose of operating, judging, promoting, and reporting on the Event (including on the Devpost page, social media, sponsor reports, and future HackSocial marketing). This license does not transfer ownership and does not grant rights to your underlying source code beyond what you make public yourself.

4.3. Your representations. You represent that your submission is your original work, does not infringe anyone's intellectual property or other rights, and does not contain unlawful, defamatory, or confidential material you are not authorized to share.

4.4. Open-source and licenses. If your project incorporates third-party or open-source materials, you are responsible for complying with their licenses.

5. Judging & Prizes

5.1. Submissions are judged by criteria published on the Devpost page (which may include impact, technical execution, originality, and completeness). Judging decisions are final.

5.2. Prizes (including any sponsor-provided credits, licenses, products, or prize tracks) are awarded at the Organizers' discretion based on judging results. Prizes are non-transferable and may not be exchanged for cash unless stated otherwise.

6. Code of Conduct

6.1. HackSocial is a harassment-free, inclusive space for everyone regardless of age, race, ethnicity, national origin, gender identity or expression, sexual orientation, disability, religion, or experience level.

6.2. The following are prohibited: harassment, hate speech, discrimination, threats, doxxing, sexual or violent content, spam, cheating, plagiarism, and any illegal activity.

6.3. Projects must not create, promote, or enable harm — including malware, tools designed for unauthorized access, harassment, discrimination, or the collection of personal data without consent.

6.4. Treat other participants, mentors, judges, sponsors, and Organizers with respect in all Event spaces (Devpost, Discord, and any video or live sessions).

6.5. To report a violation, contact the Organizers through the official Discord or the Devpost page. Organizers may warn, disqualify, or remove any participant who violates the Code of Conduct, at their sole discretion.
